# 2. Client requirement baseline and compliance

The two clients' requirements are mapped to product features or to project services. Not all are fully met; the counts per status are in §2.1, the requirements not fully met in §2.2, and the requirements whose acceptance criteria are not yet binding in §2.1 and Part 5.

## 2.1 Compliance summary

Status values come from the BA compliance review, updated where a fix closed the gap and by the reclassification against the L3 coverage (decision COMP-R5, Proposed — the Product Owner to ratify; 29 requirements, note "COMP-R5" in Part 4). "Compliant" means the P1 features listed deliver every stated part of the requirement and at least one L3 row is covered and not held by client content or a scope decision (checked by `build_l3.py --strict`). "Partially compliant" means a stated part is not delivered or has no measurable criterion; the missing part is named. "Compliant subject to client content / decision" means the delivering criteria wait on content the client has not provided or on an open decision; under §3 rule 3 they are not acceptance criteria for that requirement until resolved.

**KTDA (95 requirements)**

| Compliance status | Requirements |
|---|---|
| Compliant | 55 |
| Compliant (by inference — confirm with client) | 5 |
| Compliant subject to client content | 4 |
| Compliant subject to decision | 9 |
| Partially compliant | 5 |
| Project service — not a product feature | 15 |
| Project service — subject to decision (K-02) | 2 |
| Total | 95 |

**Malawi Post (411 requirements)**

| Compliance status | Requirements |
|---|---|
| Compliant | 315 |
| Compliant subject to client content | 17 |
| Compliant subject to decision | 34 |
| Partially compliant | 7 |
| Project service — not a product feature | 36 |
| Project service — subject to decision (C-SIZE) | 1 |
| Project service — subject to decision (K-02 / M-18) | 1 |
| Total | 411 |

**No binding criterion yet (observation).** 51 "Compliant" requirements have no binding acceptance criterion on any row that has a citing criterion (computed with the L3 rule): every such criterion is held by an architecture or licensing decision (build approach, §3.3 type b — for example WF-ENG, PAID-STUB, API-01, API-GW, DOC-LIB). Holds per requirement: REQ-KTDA-WF-001 (WF-ENG), REQ-KTDA-WF-002 (WF-ENG), REQ-KTDA-WF-003 (CALLOFF-PLACE/DOC-LIB/WF-ENG), REQ-KTDA-ORD-002 (DOC-LIB/E-INV/PH-INF), REQ-KTDA-INT-001 (API-01/API-GW/PAID-STUB), REQ-KTDA-ANL-004 (PAID-STUB), REQ-KTDA-ANL-005 (PAID-STUB), REQ-KTDA-WF-004 (WF-ENG), REQ-MPC-FR-028 (WF-ENG), REQ-MPC-FR-029 (WF-ENG), REQ-MPC-FR-031 (WF-ENG), REQ-MPC-FR-040 (WF-ENG), REQ-MPC-FR-058 (WF-ENG), REQ-MPC-FR-066 (PAID-STUB), REQ-MPC-FR-072 (API-01/API-GW/PAID-STUB), REQ-MPC-FR-090 (PAID-STUB), REQ-MPC-FR-095 (PAID-STUB), REQ-MPC-FR-097 (PAID-STUB), REQ-MPC-FR-099 (WF-ENG), REQ-MPC-FR-120 (DOC-LIB), REQ-MPC-FR-171 (DOC-LIB), REQ-MPC-FR-196 (PAID-STUB), REQ-MPC-FR-235 (WF-ENG), REQ-MPC-FR-236 (WF-ENG), REQ-MPC-FR-239 (WF-ENG), REQ-MPC-FR-240 (WF-ENG), REQ-MPC-FR-241 (WF-ENG), REQ-MPC-FR-242 (WF-ENG), REQ-MPC-FR-247 (WF-ENG), REQ-MPC-FR-276 (PAID-STUB), REQ-MPC-FR-277 (PAID-STUB), REQ-MPC-FR-278 (PAID-STUB), REQ-MPC-FR-279 (PAID-STUB), REQ-MPC-FR-280 (PAID-STUB), REQ-MPC-FR-288 (PAID-STUB), REQ-MPC-FR-294 (PAID-STUB), REQ-MPC-FR-296 (PAID-STUB), REQ-MPC-FR-300 (WF-ENG), REQ-MPC-FR-308 (WF-ENG), REQ-MPC-FR-314 (API-GW), REQ-MPC-FR-318 (WF-ENG), REQ-MPC-FR-319 (WF-ENG), REQ-MPC-FR-321 (API-GW), REQ-MPC-FR-326 (API-GW), REQ-MPC-FR-337 (API-GW), REQ-MPC-FR-338 (API-01/API-GW/PAID-STUB), REQ-MPC-FR-339 (API-GW), REQ-MPC-FR-340 (API-GW), REQ-MPC-FR-341 (API-GW), REQ-MPC-FR-346 (API-GW), REQ-MPC-FR-347 (API-01/API-GW/PAID-STUB). These decisions settle how the requirement is built, not whether it is met, so the status stays "Compliant"; none of these requirements has a binding acceptance criterion until the decision is taken.

Every requirement, with its source text, status, delivering features, implementation and notes, is in Part 4.

## 2.3 What differs between the two clients (configuration, client adapters and open scope)

Reuse scope per project instruction §5: **client-specific configuration** (settings and content on the common core) or **client-specific extension** (an adapter or item built for one client or country).

| Layer | KTDA (source) | Malawi Post (source) | Delivered by (implementation) | Reuse scope |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Branding and theme | Clean, modern, user-friendly interface aligned with KTDA branding (KTDA UX-002). Brand guidelines not provided. | Not stated in source — requires confirmation | PLT-006 (Licensed plugin) | Client-specific configuration |
| Product taxonomy | Value-added teas, bulk teas, contract packaging, forward contracts; retail and bulk (KTDA CUS-002, CAT-002, PRJ-002) | SME goods marketplace plus postal, financial and e-government services (MPC FR-026, FR-100, FR-336, FR-089); Tables 8 and 13 (services) and Table 10 (return shipping) not provided (T-MISS) | CAT-001 (Configuration); CAT-004 (Configuration); SRC-020 (Configuration); EGV-001 (Configuration) | Client-specific configuration |
| Seller model | Not stated: KTDA only, or factories and subsidiaries as sellers (gap KG-01) | Not stated: Malawi Post sells, or SMEs sell through Malawi Post (gap MG-02; inference that a marketplace may be intended) | SEL-001 (Configuration); SEL-003 (Code-level) | Client-specific configuration (Marketplace pack on/off) |
| User roles | KTDA and its stakeholders; local and international buyers (KTDA CUS-001, CUS-002); organisation structure not described (G-ORG, KG-16) | Buyers, sellers, administrative and operational users with role-based access (MPC FR-011, FR-026); named agents (FR-025); assisted registration (FR-014) | IAM-007 (Configuration); IAM-008 (Code-level); IAM-026 (Code-level) | Client-specific configuration |
| Checkout and workflow rules | Guest checkout (KTDA CUS-006); requisition workflows for custom blends, contract packaging, forward contracts (KTDA WF-001–WF-003); internal approval stages (WF-004) | Guests browse, login required before payment (MPC FR-125; conflict FG-08 handled as configuration); seller verification workflow (MPC FR-028) | CHK-002 (Configuration); ORD-015 (Code-level); ADM-002 (Code-level) | Client-specific configuration |
| Payment methods | CBK-regulated bank gateway (PAY-001), card (PAY-002), PayPal (PAY-003), M-Pesa (PAY-004), bank transfer (PAY-005), letter of credit (PAY-006) with verification before shipping (PAY-009), escrow or LC for bulk buyers (PAY-008); K-08 and C-FX open | TNM Mpamba, Airtel Money, bank transfer, card, assisted cash at outlets, cash on delivery (MPC FR-126–FR-129, FR-131, FR-132); wallet if enabled (FR-130; LIC-01) | PAY-028 (Code-level); PAY-031 (Code-level); PAY-006 (Code-level); PAY-007 (External system); PAY-029 (Code-level); PAY-030 (Code-level); PAY-032 (Code-level); PAY-008 (Code-level); PAY-009 (Code-level) | Client-specific extension |
| Language and currency | Kiswahili, English, French, Arabic, Russian, Mandarin; multi-currency with USD base (KTDA LOC-001, LOC-002); right-to-left for Arabic (inference, KG-14) | e.g. English and Chichewa (FR-023, priority Could); languages and currency not fixed (MG-04) | PLT-009 (Configuration); PLT-010 (Configuration); PLT-025 (Code-level) | Client-specific configuration (plus translation content) |

### 11.3 Traceability rules

1. Every product feature keeps its source IDs (catalog FC, KTDA, Malawi Post, Post Gebeya) in the workbook sheet "Feature sources".
2. Every L2 user story cites its PRD feature; every acceptance criterion cites its story; every test cites its acceptance criterion (L3).
3. A requirement marked "subject to decision", "subject to client content" or "by inference" is not an acceptance criterion until resolved; the L2 PRD records it as an open item.
4. For catalog lines with India examples, the capability applies with the Kenya or Malawi equivalent named by the client source; where no client source names one, it is a gap.
5. **Entry rule for Sprint Planning:** an item is taken into Sprint Planning only when it is ordered by the Product Owner, its L2 section with acceptance criteria exists and is approved, it is not held in §3.3 table 1 (holds propagate to dependants), none of its acceptance criteria taken into the Sprint is marked "held by", and every §3.3 table 2 readiness condition that names the feature is decided or its Proposed design or default is explicitly accepted by the Product Owner.
6. Gap IDs: in this document KG-nn is a KTDA gap and MG-nn a Malawi Post gap (Appendix H). In the workbook notes, "G-nn" on a KTDA row means KG-nn and on a Malawi Post row means MG-nn.
7. **Binding in L3:** the "Binding now / held by" column of L3 is generated by the same rule as L2 §5.4 — feature holds (§3.3), criterion holds ("held by"), and rule 3 for requirements subject to decision, client content or inference.

2. Every L2 user story cites its PRD feature; every acceptance criterion cites its story; every test cites its acceptance criterion (L3).
3. A requirement marked "subject to decision", "subject to client content" or "by inference" is not an acceptance criterion until resolved; the L2 PRD records it as an open item.
4. For catalog lines with India examples, the capability applies with the Kenya or Malawi equivalent named by the client source; where no client source names one, it is a gap.
5. **Entry rule for Sprint Planning:** an item is taken into Sprint Planning only when it is ordered by the Product Owner, its L2 section with acceptance criteria exists and is approved, it is not held in §3.3 table 1 (holds propagate to dependants), none of its acceptance criteria taken into the Sprint is marked "held by", and every §3.3 table 2 readiness condition that names the feature is decided or its Proposed design or default is explicitly accepted by the Product Owner.
6. Gap IDs: in this document KG-nn is a KTDA gap and MG-nn a Malawi Post gap (Appendix H). In the workbook notes, "G-nn" on a KTDA row means KG-nn and on a Malawi Post row means MG-nn.
7. **Binding in L3:** the "Binding now / held by" column of L3 is generated by the same rule as L2 §5.4 — feature holds (§3.3), criterion holds ("held by"), and rule 3 for requirements subject to decision, client content or inference.

# 5. Traceability matrix (requirement → feature → story → criterion → test; feature → story → criterion → test)


Generated from the product baseline workbook, the feature holds and the module content (`tools/trace_lib.py`). Part A links each client requirement to each product feature, its user stories, acceptance criteria and test cases (TC-<criterion>). Part B traces every product feature to its stories, criteria and tests, whether or not a client requirement cites it. Requirements: 506; Part A rows: 723; features traced: 434; stories: 1160; acceptance criteria in the product: 2850.

Modules not yet written: none.

**Binding rule (traceability rules 3 and 7).** "Not binding until X": the feature is on hold for decision X (holds propagate to dependants). "Not binding (criteria held …)": every criterion citing the requirement is marked "held by". "Not binding for this requirement until resolved": the requirement is subject to decision, client content or inference, so the criterion is not an acceptance criterion for it until resolved (the criterion may still bind for the feature). "Binding after L2 approval, except …": the listed criteria are held.

|---|---|
| Flag | Test cases are identifiers only (TC = AC, one to one). Test design (steps, data) is written by the Developers; not a PRD-level gap. |

# 6. Per-feature source traceability

For every product feature: the catalog features, client requirements and Post Gebeya items it carries, the source basis, and how each catalog line is carried. Requirement coverage by acceptance criterion is in Part 5.
